  **v31-1 — THE EMPTY FEED OFFERS WHAT'S AROUND.** The measured premise, from the tree and not the panel: the empty feed rendered **no filled control at all** and **no door to `/browse`** (`FeedPage.tsx:1371-1375`; the only browse door was the shell's nav tab), while the panel's parents reached for exactly that. One new prop on the shared `RadiusEmptyState` (`showBrowseCta`, default **false**) + a headline and a label pinned in `lib/feed.ts` with a sibling test (label, headline, no digit, no place category, no content promise). **Browse is unchanged on purpose** — a browse → browse link is a control that cannot do anything — and the spec asserts that too. **V27's ruling is untouched: `showPostCta` stays false, so no post CTA was added.** Evidence: `npx playwright test e2e/feed-empty-state.e2e.ts` → **8 passed (58.5s)** including the new click-through test; mobile audit exit 0 (§ below).
  **v31-2 — THE NIGHTLY LANE'S UNEXPLAINED FAILURE WAS CONFIG, NOT A TEST.** `e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts:489` ("Notifications are on") had failed every night since **2026-10-03** because **CI built a keyless bundle**: the workflow wrote `.env` from two repository variables and never carried `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, so `subscribe()` was called without an `applicationServerKey`, the app refused honestly (`pushClient.ts:506-543`), V28 r3-2's hardened stub refused too, no row was written, and `push-status` could never say "on". **A/B proven, only the key differing:** keyless → **1 failed / 8 passed, EXIT=1**; keyed → **9 passed, EXIT=0**. Four competing hypotheses **ruled out by evidence** (live-DB state, a v29-1 regression, order dependence, timeout). **Fixed:** the key is now a repository variable (set 2026-10-05 — public key, same reasoning as the anon key), `e2e-scheduled.yml` carries it in all four env blocks + its `.env` write + its configuration gate, and a missing key now **skips the lane with a notice** instead of failing it as a mystery. `docs/agents/ci.md` documents the third variable and the two days it was missing. **The lesson, and it is the verify gate's lesson again: a failure whose symptom is indistinguishable from a flake is a failure nobody diagnoses.**
  **v31-3 — THE CONFIG NUMBER AND ITS PINNED ASSERTION AGREE.** `strata-max` declared `ram_gb: 55` while **its own `footprint_evidence` said 52 was the measured figure and 55 was the unit banner's estimate** (and `compute-policy.md`'s own arithmetic, `52 + 4 + 3 = 59`, already used 52). Config → **52**, and the four assertions that encoded the old arithmetic moved in the same diff (62 → **59**, 61 → **58** twice, the pinned `/RAM: needs 62 GB/` → **59**) plus the machine fixture and every comment restating the sum. `npx vitest run scripts/factory/scheduler.test.mjs` → **42 passed**; the open item in `compute-policy.md` is closed with its evidence. **The plan's own verification line was wrong and was caught before dispatch** — that file is a vitest suite, not a plain node script.
  **v31-4 — THE BUILD CAN NO LONGER SHIP THE DEV INJECTOR (found while gathering v31-1's evidence, fixed because it recurred).** The mobile audit failed **56 checks** — all of them the impeccable design tool's own overlay (Pick element / Bolder / Distill / Voice input…). Cause, measured: the tool writes a commented `<script src="http://localhost:8400/live.js?token=…">` block into **`index.html`, a TRACKED file** (uncommitted, machine-local), and `vite build` baked it into `dist/` — so the preview served the overlay, and **the audit measured the dev tool instead of the app**. The V29 entry flagged this same injector and stripped it **by hand**; it came back the moment the tool ran again, which is the whole argument against hand-stripping. **Fixed at the build instead of by hand:** a build-only `transformIndexHtml` plugin (`vite.config.ts`, `stripDevInjector`) removes the block from the built HTML while the dev server keeps serving it, so the human's tooling still works. **Proven, not assumed:** with the block present in `index.html` (2 markers), `npm run build` → `dist/index.html` contains **0** occurrences of `impeccable-live` and **0** of `localhost:8400`; the audit then runs clean. **The human's working-tree edit is deliberately NOT reverted** — committing or reverting another session's in-flight work is their call, and the tag is now harmless to `dist/`. ⚠️ **It is still in the working tree and still stageable by a careless `git add -A`** — this commit excludes `index.html` and the file is named here so the next pusher checks it.

  **v31-5 — THE THREE MODERATOR SPECS RUN ON A PROJECT-SCOPED KEY (founder's option 3, 2026-10-05).** The three specs that made the nightly unrunnable-goal-red for a *fourth* reason (`37321138731`: 214ms/166ms/272ms failures) went through `runSqlHeadless` → `scripts/db-sql.sh` → the Supabase **Management API**, whose `sbp_` token is ACCOUNT-level. The transport is now typed **PostgREST calls authenticated with the project-scoped service-role key**, read from the repo `.env` exactly as the other values are; `runSqlHeadless` and `sqlLiteral` are deleted (zero callers); `scripts/db-sql.sh` is untouched and keeps its management token for migrations; `runLiveSql` stays for `polish.e2e.ts`. Each write asks for `return=representation` and **verifies its own read-back**, so a PATCH that matched zero rows is loud rather than a silent no-op; a missing key throws `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY missing from …/.env` **before any request**; and the request headers never enter a failure message, so the key cannot leak into a test log. **CI:** the key is a repository **secret** (set 2026-10-05 — it bypasses RLS, unlike the other three, so it is not a `VITE_` variable and not a variable at all), carried in all four workflow env blocks, written into `.env`, and named by the skip-gate so an unconfigured lane SKIPS loudly instead of failing three mysteries. **EVIDENCE, re-run by the orchestrator and not taken from the builder:** `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN= npx playwright test e2e/moderator-door.e2e.ts e2e/place-photo-admin.e2e.ts` → **4 passed (21.1s)** — the management token is *proven absent* from that path, which is the whole point; live read-back after the run shows the marker `moderators: false`, Green Lake Park's five photo columns `null`, and **no e2e stray moderator** (only the founder's own account is flagged); `npm run verify` → **exit 0, 75 files / 2197 tests / 86 warnings / 0 errors / GUARDS PASS**. **⚠️ THE PLAN'S OWN ACCEPTANCE CRITERION WAS WRONG AND THE BUILDER REFUSED TO FUDGE IT:** it demanded zero hits for the service key's **first 24 characters** in `dist/`, which is 1 and can never be 0 — the anon and service-role keys are both legacy JWTs sharing the base64url of `{"alg":"HS256","typ":"JWT"}`. The corrected probes (orchestrator-run, with controls) are **0 hits** for the full key, its 24-character signature tail, `service_role` and the variable name, while the same probes **hit** for the public anon key; no tracked file contains the full key either.   **⚠️ TWO VERCEL PROJECTS — AND THIS REPO WAS LINKED TO THE WRONG ONE.** The production URL `drop-in-mu.vercel.app` belongs to a project named **`drop-in`**. `.vercel/project.json` pointed at a **duplicate project named `playdate-app`** (`playdate-app-pink.vercel.app`), so a bare `npx vercel --prod` run from this repo deploys the duplicate and leaves the founder's URL untouched — which is exactly what the first attempt did. **Fixed by construction:** the local link is now `drop-in` (`npx vercel link --project drop-in`), so a bare `vercel --prod` in this repo hits the right project.   **🔴 THE FORGE GATE WAS RED ON 16 CONSECUTIVE MASTER PUSHES OVER 29.9 HOURS (2026-10-03T20:54Z → 2026-10-05T02:48Z) — FOUND AND FIXED (`50eed5b`, first green run `success`).** Nobody had recorded it, which is the actual damage: master had been **green** before the streak (three green pushes earlier the same day, 18:58/19:27/19:39Z), a red X then became the normal state, and CI enforced **nothing across those 16 pushes** while real work shipped. The streak begins exactly when the guard reached master — `check-acceptance-greps` is absent from `scripts/guards/run-all.sh` at `ee0e233` (0 matches) and present at `62f7b78` (3), the merge that landed it. **Two environment defects in `.github/workflows/verify.yml`, and zero code defects** — every guard was making a correct finding:
  **(1) `check-acceptance-greps` shells out to `rg`, and the ubuntu runner does not ship ripgrep.** Every tagged planning claim resolved to `spawnSync rg ENOENT`, so it failed 4 claims **and 9 of its own 14 behavior checks** — the guard was doing exactly the right thing and the environment was wrong. Fixed by installing ripgrep in the workflow (silencing the guard was the wrong fix).
  **(2) `e2e-target-guard` resolves the live Supabase ref by reading the `.env` FILE, not the process environment**, so passing `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`/`ANON_KEY` as `env:` was never enough — it reported `Cannot read .env`. Fixed by writing the gitignored `.env` from the same two repository variables, the same step `e2e-scheduled.yml` already used for the same reason.
  **LESSON, and it belongs with the others:** a gate whose failure is indistinguishable from its baseline is not a gate. "CI is red" was visible on every push and nobody looked, because looking had stopped paying. The check that would have caught it is the one the repo already believes in — **a claim needs its evidence, and "our CI runs the gate" was a claim nobody had re-measured since 2026-10-03.**

Three of the four are the lane's own test failures; **the fourth, added here as "unexplained", turned out to be CONFIG, not a test at all** (see its entry below — corrected 2026-10-05, so this paragraph is not read as "all four are genuine"):
  - `e2e/places-map-view.e2e.ts:574` (the map view's `aria-current`) — **failed the night before as well** (`:567`, before the line moved): **persistent, not a flake**.
  - `e2e/places.e2e.ts:917` — the **named known flake** in `RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md:277-278`.
  - `e2e/places.e2e.ts:1674` — **fails at the base commit `580eb82`** (proven in a throwaway worktree at v29-11): inherited, not a regression.
  - `e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts:489` (`Notifications are on`) — ✅ **DIAGNOSED AND FIXED 2026-10-05 (V31 v31-2). It was NOT a test failure at all: it was a missing CI variable.** The nightly workflow wrote `.env` from **two** repository variables and never carried `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`, so CI built a **keyless** bundle; the app refuses to call `subscribe()` without an `applicationServerKey` (`src/lib/pushClient.ts:506-543`), V28 r3-2's hardened stub refuses the keyless call too (`e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts:202-210`), no `push_subscriptions` row is ever written, and `push-status` can therefore never say "on" (`NotificationsSection.tsx:181,361-363`). **Reproduced on demand, A/B, only the key differing:** `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY='' npx playwright test e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts --reporter=list` → **1 failed / 8 passed, EXIT=1**, received `"Notifications are off.…"`; the same command with the key → **9 passed, EXIT=0**. **RULED OUT by evidence:** live-DB state (a leftover row would fail the EARLIER "off" assert, the marker is fresh per run, the read is profile-scoped, the only unique key is the per-run `endpoint`); a v29-1 regression (the failing run predates `dbb02cb`, which touched neither `NotificationsSection` nor `pushClient`, and the test passes at HEAD with the key); order dependence (fresh context per test, deterministic first try); and timeout (15s expect, value unchanged across 33 polls — a permanent state). **THE FIX:** `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is now a repository variable (set 2026-10-05, public key — same reasoning as the anon key) and `e2e-scheduled.yml` carries it in all four env blocks, its `.env` write, and its configuration gate — so a missing key now **skips the lane with a notice** instead of failing it confusingly. `docs/agents/ci.md` documents the third variable and the two days it was missing. **⚠️ The other three failures are unchanged and are NOT this:** they are the lane's own batch. **The lesson is the same one the verify gate taught two entries up: a failure whose symptom is indistinguishable from a flake is a failure nobody diagnoses — and this one was a CONFIG gap wearing a test's clothes.**

Create an account"* sat at **640–692 — 28px past the bottom edge** of a centered auth page that gives no hint scrolling would reveal it. **The audit could not have caught it, and that was the real defect:** `scripts/mobile-audit.mjs` asserted **horizontal** overflow only and had no 390×664 viewport. It now has both, plus **two containment claims that ask different questions** — *portrait, on a page that claims to fit one screen* (`/login`): every control inside the initial viewport; *any viewport*: every control **reachable by scrolling** (landscape phones legitimately scroll; demanding a full sign-in form fit 390px of height would be a worse design). **The guard was proven able to fire BEFORE the fix**: it reported exactly `below the fold: button "New here? (b) **the sign-out spec signed the MARKER out**, and `supabase.auth.signOut()` revokes the refresh token **globally**, so the saved marker state every later spec reads was dead and **~15 specs met a `/login` screen that had nothing to do with them** — fixed with a **throwaway account in its own context**. **The instrument that separated "my regression" from "inherited flake" was a throwaway worktree at the base commit `580eb82`: the failing specs PASSED there.** **SECOND FULL RUN: 181 passed / 3 failed / 2 skipped, 20.1m** — all three in `places.e2e.ts` (Leaflet), and **nothing in the three is this batch's**: `:917` and `:1674` **fail at the base commit too** (proven in that worktree, now removed), and `:2792` **passes in isolation at HEAD and at base** (intermittent map flake). **`npm run verify` EXIT=0 throughout (73 files / 2139 tests / 81 warnings / 0 errors / GUARDS PASS); `node scripts/mobile-audit.mjs` EXIT=0** across 7 viewports × light/dark × 3 routes. **⚠️ `ocr` LANE UNAVAILABLE** — not installed on this box (not on PATH, not in `node_modules/.bin`); recorded as a deviation, **never as a pass**. **MARKER RESIDUE, RECORDED NOT DELETED:** `sweep-e2e-markers.mjs select` reports **264 marker profiles/auth.users + 5 kids (533 rows)** with **every content table at ZERO** — accounts accumulate by design and deleting them needs fresh human confirmation. **NOT DONE, deliberately not mine:** **v29-0's second half** (committing the pre-batch WIP: `AGENTS.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, `docs/RELEASE-CHECKLIST.md`, `docs/agents/e2e-fixture-convention.md`, `scripts/guards/run-all.sh` — the `index.html` injector half IS done, `c6935a3`), the **destructive marker sweep**, **deleting `.vercel-env-tmp.json` and rotating its OIDC token** (now gitignored, `f7041dd`), the **form-draft design decision** (option A/B/C), and the **5-parent first-open test v29-1 unblocked**. **r3-7/r3-8 stay carried forward** in `plan.md` §6 — the IN-APP half of "what is a drop-in" is still unbuilt.
  **✅ v29-6 AND v29-7 ARE DONE (commits `5b74862`, `19ac6e4`, 2026-10-04) — the feed stops lying about what is out there, and stops hiding your own post.** **v29-6:** the empty state now carries a second line — *"N drop-ins are further out, within 35 miles."* — quoting **the same ceiling the "See everything" escape writes**, and rendering **nothing at all at 0** (an empty city is not sold a number). It rides the **existing** read: `queryUpcomingPlaydates` already returned every upcoming post and the radius filter is what discarded them, so **`listRadiusFeed` now returns `{ posts, beyondRadiusCount }`** (one caller, no mocks) and the count runs **the same `filterFeed` at `RADIUS_MAX_MILES` and subtracts** — so it can never promise what a blocked host, an ended post or an unplaceable post would hide. **v29-7:** `filterFeed` gained **`ownProfileId`**: a viewer's **own** post is exempt from their own radius **and from nothing else** (blocked, hidden, ended and the "coordinates are never invented" exclusion all still apply — the exemption is about **distance**). **GATES: `npm run verify` EXIT=0 on both — 73 files / 2135 tests / 81 warnings / 0 errors / GUARDS PASS** (`.scratch/v29-6-verify.txt`, `.scratch/v29-7-verify.txt`). **BROWSER CHECKS: `feed-empty-state` 7 passed** (`.scratch/v29-6-e2e.txt`) — the new rendered test posts from a **second account at 98007**, deliberately NOT the marker's own post, because v29-7 makes own posts visible at any distance and would otherwise have broken it the moment it landed; **`zip-radius` 4 passed** (`.scratch/v29-7-e2e.txt`) — posts at the furthest seeded place (**Lakeridge Park, ~12.5 mi**), then **narrows the radius to 1 mile** and proves the card is still there and the empty state is not. **ONE FAILURE WORTH KEEPING:** the first v29-7 attempt failed because **the place directory is RADIUS-FILTERED from the viewer's own profile**, so a 1-mile radius left the picker with no rows; the spec now patches **wide (35), posts, then narrows (1)** — a fact the next spec that drives the directory inherits, and it is in the ledger. Still built inline; still 5 slices + the batch close to go (v29-4, v29-5, v29-8, v29-9, v29-10, v29-11). **v29-0 remains open** (pre-batch WIP unstaged, `index.html` injector), and every e2e run keeps adding one marker ACCOUNT that only the sweep can remove — which needs the human's confirmation.

- **⏸️ PARKED, DELIBERATELY — SHIPPING DropIn AS A NATIVE iOS + ANDROID APP (2026-09-30). NOTHING WAS STARTED, AND NOTHING SHOULD HAVE BEEN.** The founder's decision: DropIn ships as an installed app on both stores, and that becomes the primary way parents use it — because **iOS Web Push only works if the parent adds the web app to their Home Screen**, and most will not. A real installed app gets APNs/FCM with no install ritual, which removes the biggest adoption cliff in the product.
  **📄 READ FIRST: `docs/handoff-native-apps.md`** — written FOR THE FOUNDER to pick up cold months from now, and deliberately a different document from the executable plan. **`.scratch/native-apps/plan.md`** has the slices, acceptance criteria and verification commands. The handoff carries what the plan does not: the decision and its reasoning, four code hazards with line numbers, the risks that decide success, and **a "what will have rotted" table** — re-verify that table before starting, because this repo moved **86 commits** while this session was away.
  **WHY IT IS PARKED, NOT BLOCKED:** the app is **not feature-complete**, and a native shell is one of the LAST things to add — every feature built after it means re-syncing the shell and re-testing on device. All four paid/irreversible steps are therefore **deferred on purpose**: the Mac, Apple Developer ($99/yr — an annual subscription, so starting early burns months of it), Google Play ($25), and both APNs/FCM credentials. **Do not start them early.**
  **THE FOUR SURPRISES NOW WRITTEN DOWN, so they are not discovered at submission:** (1) **this box is Linux and CANNOT build iOS** — no `xcodebuild`, no CocoaPods — while Java 26 and a full `~/Android/Sdk` ARE present, which is why the plan is **ANDROID-FIRST**; the iOS route (GitHub Actions `macos-latest` on a **private** repo, so macOS minutes bill at 10× ≈ 200/month, a borrowed Mac, or a paid cloud Mac) is the founder's call and they have said they will buy a Mac. (2) **Apple guideline 4.2 rejects repackaged websites** — mitigated by **bundling `dist/`** (only 1.5 MB) rather than loading a remote URL, which means the shell must NOT be a live-URL wrapper. (3) **Native push must join the EXISTING drain as a third branch**, never a second Edge Function — two functions draining `sent_at is null` race each other for the same rows. (4) **FOUR code sites assume a browser origin and will break in a shell**: `src/lib/db.ts:317` (OAuth `redirectTo`), `src/lib/db.ts:1352` (**password-reset** `redirectTo`), `src/lib/db.ts:1339` (share URL), `src/lib/email.ts:40` (base-URL fallback). Each needs a *"what is the canonical public URL inside the app"* decision — a judgment call, not a mechanical fix, which is why it is not done here. **Cheap now, confusing during submission.**
  **NOT PARKED, AND WORTH REMEMBERING:** the web app STAYS as the link landing pad and install funnel — password-reset links, email confirmation, shared drop-in links and Google's OAuth consent screen all assume a browser exists. Verified facts at the time of writing, all of which will rot: Capacitor **8.5.2**, `@capacitor/push-notifications` **8.1.2**, next free migration **0061**, **EIGHT** notification kinds, `send-push` **v8**, and the email fallback working in production (**60 `sent:email` rows**).

- **🔔 V28 INCIDENT — THE FIXTURE EMAILS, THE LOST PRODUCER, AND THE CLOBBERED FORMATTER (founder-reported, fixed live 2026-09-30).**
  **The report:** *"i've been getting tons of these emails … i want them to stop"* — 21 `followed_new_dropin` emails to `jonmeisburg@gmail.com` in two days, every one a Playwright fixture (`"e2e e2e-1790760927 Marker stale neighbourhood"`).
  **Cause, measured not guessed:** the founder follows the place `Green Lake Park`; three specs hardcode `PLACE_NAME = 'Green Lake Park'` (`e2e/post-location.e2e.ts:85`, `e2e/places.e2e.ts:101`, `e2e/post-fast.e2e.ts:101`) and `playwright.config.ts` drives the **live** project; the `notification_followed_new_dropin` trigger fanned every fixture out to the place's followers; cron job 3 (`*/5`, active) drained it; he has **0** registered devices, so `emailFallback` sent email. He is the **only** follower of that place — 3 of 239 places have any follower, all Meisburg accounts — so **no outside family was affected**.
  **⚠️ TWO DEFECTS BEHIND THE NOISE, both found by chasing the symptom to the database:**
  **(1) REPO↔PRODUCTION DRIFT.** `notify_followed_new_dropin`, its trigger, the `followed_new_dropin` kind and its CHECK value existed **only live** — no migration in the repo, on any branch (`git log -S` → nothing; every other public function is in the repo). Its own comments number themselves "0052 pin e"/"pin h", and repo 0052 is the reviews migration: written as 0052 in a workspace, applied live, lost in the V27 renumber. **`0060` is that migration, recovered verbatim from the live schema.**
  **(2) A REAL LIVE PRODUCT DEFECT.** Because the lost migration was applied AFTER 0055/0056, its older `notification_payload` overwrote both — so live, `review_due` and `new_message` fell through to `else 'Drop In'` / `else ''`. Proven against production: **every one of the 8 `new_message` rows rendered `title='Drop In'`, EMPTY body, and the drop-in url instead of `/inbox?thread=`** (the V27 Inbox feature's copy). `review_due` was inert only by luck — the sender builds those rows in TS. Blast radius: 8 broken rows, all from the day's e2e run, **zero real recipients**.
  **THE FIX — `0060_e2e_fixture_guard_and_payload_restore.sql`, applied live (HTTP 201).** One file, three repairs: (a) **the fixture guard** — `public.is_e2e_profile(uuid)` (account marker `email like 'e2e-%'`, the convention's primary handle, fail-OPEN on an unreadable read like `emailFallback`'s opt-out rule) called by **ALL FIVE producers** (`notify_ping_received`, `notify_new_comment`, `notify_playdate_cancelled`, `notify_new_message`, `notify_followed_new_dropin`) — because the same log already held a fixture `ping_received` on a REAL post (2026-09-24) and a fixture `cancelled` (2026-09-20), so fixing one door would have left four open; (b) **the recovered producer + trigger** (`WHEN (new.series_id is null)` kept verbatim); (c) **the formatter restored as the 7-parameter union** — all EIGHT branches char-for-char, with the 5-argument signature DROPPED so a stray call cannot silently lose the two branches.
  **EVIDENCE (all fresh, this session):** a **dry run** inside `begin; … rollback;` → HTTP 201, then a read proving nothing persisted; the real apply → HTTP 201; the migration's own **read-back DO block** asserts the 8-kind CHECK, the exact signature/flags, every branch's rendered strings, the 5-arg signature being GONE, the guard's **functional** both-ways behaviour, all five producers calling it, and the trigger's presence; **the rolled-back functional probe** (`probe-0060-live.sql`, output `.scratch/v28/probe-0060-live.txt`) measured **fixture post at a followed place → 0 notifications** (was 1), **real-host control at the same place → 1** (the guard is not over-broad), **fixture ping on a real post → 0**; a post-probe read proved **0 playdates / 0 notifications / 0 pings** left behind; and a **before/after dump of all six live functions** shows the producers changed by **exactly the guard clause** (`.scratch/v28/before/` vs `after/`).
  **REPO SIDE, so it cannot drift twice:** `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` and `EMAIL_KINDS` widened to eight (the drift guard in `src/lib/email.test.ts` demanded it), the missing TS twin `followed_new_dropin` added to `pushCopy.ts` (+ `whenLabel()`, built from `Intl` PARTS to match the SQL's `FMDy FMHH12:MIam` char-for-char), `NOTIFICATION_KIND_COPY` gained the settings row (the `Record<NotificationKind, …>` type is what turned the new kind into a **compile error** — the second one caught it: `src/lib/push.ts:63`), tests added, and rule **5 + a "Side effects the sweep cannot undo" section** written into `docs/agents/e2e-fixture-convention.md`.   **✅ RESOLVED — THE SENDER DEPLOY IS APPLIED (`send-push` → v8, 2026-09-30T14:32:08Z).** The founder's yes was given (*"yes do what you think is best"*), then `npx supabase@2.118.0 functions deploy send-push --project-ref ayzvjwxyrcgyoeaxuk` → EXIT=0. **Three signals prove it:** `version` **7→8**, `updated_at` 1790555535701→**2026-09-30T14:32:08Z**, `ezbr_sha256` `bf329a7d…`→**`e74fbe46…`**; `verify_jwt: true` and `ACTIVE` unchanged; and **`prefill-playdate` is untouched (v5, same sha)** — the named function only, not an accidental "deploy all". **The deployed ARTIFACT carries the eighth kind:** the ESZip pulled back from the management API (`.scratch/v28/deployed/send-push.eszip`, 9.4 MB, HTTP 200) contains `followed_new_dropin` — a string that existed nowhere in this repo's functions before this change. **THE NEW BUNDLE IS RUNNING IN PRODUCTION, WITH REAL DATA:** the 14:35:00Z cron tick returned HTTP 200 `{"ok":true,…,"rows":1,"sent":1}` — and that row is a **real parent's real alert**, not a probe: the founder's own post *"Drop-in 2"* at a real place (created 14:33:57, `is_e2e_profile → false`) produced a `followed_new_dropin` row for `nicolemeisburg@gmail.com`, delivered by email at 14:35:04 (`error = 'sent:email'`). Three things proven at once by a thing I did not stage: the guard is **not over-broad** (a real follower still gets a real alert), the recovered producer **works**, and the new sender **runs**.
  **ALSO DONE (his "do what you think is best"): his alert history is cleaned.** 23 fixture-origin rows deleted from `notification_log` for `jonmeisburg@gmail.com` — 21 `followed_new_dropin` (every one whose playdate no longer exists) + 1 fixture `cancelled` + 1 fixture `ping_received` (9/24, the fixture pinger on his REAL post).   **EIGHTEEN PLAN DEFECTS have been found and fixed — essentially all by measuring the tree instead of trusting the plan's prose.** The recurring class, three times now: **a sweep that names one file has not proven there is only one** (`feed.ts:49`, `db.ts:577`, and the `V20 t06` stale comment which the reviewer found once and the orchestrator found in **four** e2e files). The plan's own instruction for such claims is now written as a **grep, not a list**, because a list is provably incomplete.
  **THE MEASURED TRUTH ABOUT A NO-ZIP PARENT (this is why 2c exists).** `db.ts:582` — `if (viewer.homeZip === null) return []`. So the feed query returns an empty list, `posts.length === 0` fires, and **`RadiusEmptyState` renders with every escape button disabled and the post CTA suppressed** — a lie ("Nothing within N miles yet.") whose only controls are inert. **Slice 2b is what made it reachable;** before it, the wall kept a no-zip parent off the feed. It is also reachable on **Browse**, via `PlaceDirectory.tsx:1117`.
  **THE BATCH'S CORE INVARIANT, PINNED:** *the location requirement must never be absent at a slice boundary.* Slice 2a adds the guards **before** Slice 2b removes the wall — so what 2b takes off the app-wide gate lands on the four write paths. Removing the wall first would have **removed** the requirement rather than **moved** it. Every later brief must attest where the requirement lives in its report.
  **FOUR DEFECTS IN THE ORCHESTRATOR'S OWN WORK, ALL CAUGHT BY THE LANE — the review process is earning its keep.** (1) the resume rule's prose was self-contradictory (clause (b) alone never terminates for a childless parent); (2) a reviewer's factual citation was **false** (it claimed `src/lib/a11y.ts` ends in a newline; a 40-file sample splits 25 with / 15 without) — *a reviewer's citations need adjudication as much as its reasoning*; (3) Slice 2a's brief said "the going-ping action" (singular) because it grepped **handler names** rather than the write function's **call sites** — `togglePing(` has **three** call sites in pages, so two write paths would have proceeded with no location after 2b; (4) the orchestrator's own grep printed "no matches" **from an error path** (`rg -E` errored), which looked like a clean result. Lessons recorded in `plan.md` and the ledger: *a guard is only as complete as your grep for the call sites of the thing being guarded*, and *a no-match produced by an error path is not a no-match*.
  **PROCESS RULE ADOPTED (after the reviewer noted it): a claim in the ledger is not evidence.** The ledger carries **actual output tails** for gate claims, or the claim goes to a **verifier lane**. Slice 1's closing verification used this to catch a brief that was wrong rather than a slice that was (the "nothing outside the slice moved" check flagged the orchestrator's own record commits — scope checks must be limited to source/test/script/config paths).

  **THE FINDING THAT REFRAMED THE ASK.** The human asked for "an onboarding flow… like most apps have." **`/onboarding` already ships** (582 lines, V2→V20): name → home ZIP + radius (required, gates every protected route) → photo + bio + kids (optional). The real defect is structural: **signup already collects first name, last name and a required home address, geocodes it into `home_zip`, and then navigates straight to `/` (`LoginPage.tsx:239`) — never through `/onboarding`.** So the "tell us about your family" step was only ever offered to parents whose address *failed* to geocode. That is a routing bug, not a missing flow.
  **AND THE APP HAS NO USERS TO MEASURE.** Measured against the live DB: of 54 non-`e2e` profiles, **2 are human** (`jonmeisburg@`, `nicolemeisburg@`) — the rest are `slice9-*`/`testaccount` fixtures. **16 drop-ins exist, the latest `2026-09-26`; zero are upcoming** — *a dated snapshot, measured 2026-09-29 and accurate when written; the count has since moved to 20, and the current measurement is in the r2 entry above.* Enrichment is thin: 7/54 bios, 10/54 photos. So "increases adoption and reduces churn" cannot be tested here; this is recorded as a **launch-prep bet**, and the honest success test is *Nicole completes the flow unassisted on her phone*.
  **FIFTEEN DECISIONS SETTLED BY GRILLING (do not re-litigate in a builder):** launch prep / learn-about-you-then-land-on-something-real / the ZIP gate moves off the app and onto the writes / **account first as card 1 of 5** / **account → name → kids → photo → area → finish** / resume at the card they left, never a wall / cold start = real places nearby **plus** hand-seeded drop-ins / **name + area required, kids + photo skippable** / the **area card** asks the address with ZIP fallback / V25's push prompt keeps owning notifications / kids card keeps names + ages (a kid's name is already gated by V4/V9 and labels "who's coming") / the ending is its **own** places-near-you card, not `/browse` / one batch, sliced, playtest after the cards / **the human seeds the real drop-ins by hand** / **bio drops out of the first run** (7/54 have one; V27's parent card already owns that editor).
  **ONE DESIGN CALL MADE WITHOUT ASKING, FLAGGED:** **resume is DERIVED, not stored** — no step column, no migration. The rule is pinned in the plan's Interfaces as two clauses: **(a) if every required card is answered the run is finished (`null`); (b) otherwise the first card in order that is unanswered and is either required, or optional with every required card before it answered.** Clause (a) is load-bearing — without it the rule never terminates for a childless parent. Its accepted cost is that a *skipped* optional card is re-offered while the run is unfinished, because "skipped" and "not reached" are indistinguishable from derived facts. Reversing that means adding a column, which is out of scope.
  **A REUSE THE PLAN LEANS ON:** `profiles` has **no auto-create trigger** — `createProfile(displayName)` is called explicitly from `/login` and requires a name. So card 1 (email + password only) cannot create the row, and **card 2 (name) IS the branch `/onboarding` already has for first-time social sign-in**. Generalize it; do not rewrite it.
  **STAGED BY DESIGN (decision 13):** one batch, sliced. A playtest checkpoint exists mid-batch, and **Slice 2 (moving the gate) is declared cross-cutting** — a builder that cannot land the feed, the detail page and the host page inside one context must **stop and report**, because a half-moved gate is worse than the wall it replaces.

  **Fix round 1 (`c253865`, inbox + lightbox):** `mergeConversations` keyed every row by `otherPartyId`, which `db.ts` sets to `''` when the counterpart cannot be resolved — so every such conversation collapsed into ONE list row, the others were **dropped from the inbox entirely**, and their unread counts were summed onto the survivor. Unresolved rows are now keyed per conversation, with the two-empty-id unit test that nothing constructed before. Separately, `openGallery` clamped its index against the caller's pre-filter array while storing the filtered one, opening a different photo than the one tapped.
  **Fix round 2 (`5c977ca`, notifications):** `enablePush()` remembered a dismissed OS dialog as the **global** `'dismissed'` decision, and the seam treats `'dismissed'` as permanent silence — so a parent who tapped "Turn on" and then dismissed the OS box **lost the other two prompts forever**, which is the exact bug class the t15 slice exists to fix. The `/settings` off-switch is now the only writer of `'dismissed'`. The "when is a point spent" rule moved out of `PushOptInPrompt.tsx` into the pure `shouldSpendPushPoint` (build law: a rule that lives only in a `.tsx` is unreachable from the vitest lane), and a marker-versioned `migrateLegacyDecisionOnce` resets a legacy `'dismissed'` once so existing parents are not silently locked out of all three moments. The test that pinned the old global behaviour was **updated, not deleted**, and the new tests were **mutation-checked** (an identity `migrateLegacyDecision` fails 3; dropping the path rules fails 2).

(It freed up later; the other lane's run simply ended.)
  **PASS — `verify-pwa.mjs`:** service worker takes control, manifest `Drop In` / `display: standalone`, all three icons 200 (192, 512, maskable-512), `apple-mobile-web-app-capable: yes`, **8 Apple startup images**, and a cold **offline** load still paints the app shell. This is the iOS *install* surface — the closest thing to objective item 3 that is testable without the founder's iPad.
  **PASS — `verify-splash.mjs`:** splash at 180 ms, gone by 1531 ms, `withinCap`, app usable after, and it does not replay on nav.
  **PASS — `theme-contract-check.mjs`:** light is the default for everyone, dark is opt-in, and a stored-dark cold load paints dark on the FIRST frame (no flash).
  **FALSE-FAIL — `layout-width-check.mjs` (13 checks).** Every failure is a **nav** assertion: `nav nullxnull null`. The script navigates signed-out routes, where `/playdate/:id` redirects to `/login` and there is **no nav element**; its own header even says *"The signed-out shell is what is measurable without credentials."* Every non-nav check passes (`overflow 0px`, `content wider than the phone measure`). **The lane asserts a property it cannot measure on the routes it visits.**
  **FALSE-FAIL — `dark-mode-check.mjs` (1 check).** The failing assertion is `brightCards.length > 0` on `/login`. That route has exactly two opaque surfaces: the cream shell `rgb(251,247,244)` and a **terracotta** brand fill `rgb(232,85,47)`. The "bright" test is `r+g+b > 400`, and terracotta sums to **364** — so no bright card is found and the guard fails. The card selector also only considers `div, section, article`, while the white elements on `/login` are `<button>`s. **The real appearance contract all passes**: light-by-default under emulated OS dark (the regression that slice exists to pin), `data-theme` not dark without a stored choice, stored-dark renders dark, no bright card on the dark page, and 6.63:1 text contrast.
  **NOT MINE, and verified so:** the files changed across this batch are email/SMTP/docs/e2e only — **nothing touches `/login`** — and `src/pages/LoginPage.tsx` was last modified by `9082415`, the previous day.
  **DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED.** Both are false *failures* (safe, not false passes), and editing a verification lane to go green is precisely the pattern `config-guard` exists to stop — `scripts/dark-mode-check.mjs` is not in its protected list, so nothing would have stopped me, which is exactly why it needs a human. **Proposed fixes, one word to approve:** (a) `layout-width-check` should SKIP its nav assertions when signed out, keeping its overflow/measure ones; (b) `dark-mode-check` should assert *"if a card surface exists it is bright"* plus a separate non-vacuity guard on a surface that DOES exist (the terracotta brand fill or the shell), instead of demanding bright cards on a route that has none.

- **🚀 WHY `git push` STOPPED DEPLOYING — THE DELETED GITHUB ACCOUNT BROKE VERCEL'S COMMIT-AUTHOR CHECK, AND THE FIX IS THE AUTHOR EMAIL (2026-09-26, diagnosed and fixed).** `drop-in-mu.vercel.app` kept serving the old bundle after two pushes. Vercel's own `vercel inspect` gave the reason verbatim:
  **`The deployment was blocked because Vercel couldn't find a Git account for the commit author.`**
  The deployment list proves it — same author *email* throughout, but only the pre-deletion commits resolve to a GitHub login:
  | Time | State | SHA | Author Vercel resolved |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 10:29 | **BLOCKED** | `557ce76` | `Jon Meisburg` (a display name — nothing to map to) |
  | 09:44 | **BLOCKED** | `74b12fd` | `Jon Meisburg` |
  | 00:28 | READY | `7921429` | **`Meisburg`** |
  | 23:33 | READY | `1508e53` | **`Meisburg`** |
  **Cause:** `user.email` was `jonmeisburg+github@gmail.com`, an address belonging to the **deleted** GitHub account. It resolved to `Meisburg` while that account existed; after the deletion Vercel can no longer map it and **blocks the build rather than shipping an unattributable commit.**
  **Fix:** commit as the live account's GitHub *noreply* address —
  `git config user.email "331023862+Meisburg@users.noreply.github.com"`
  (331023862 is `Meisburg`'s id; `gh api user --jq '{id,login}'` gives both). Set repo-locally. **Every future commit must carry a mappable author or it will be silently blocked.**
  **✅ VERIFIED FIXED, 2026-09-26.** Pushed `012f1cd` authored as the noreply address; Vercel built it **● Ready in 21s** where the previous two sat **● Blocked**. `drop-in-mu.vercel.app` then served a **new** bundle (`index-CZGdvD0M.js`, previously `index-DJRfmYow.js`) containing **`email-optout`**, and `/reset-password` still returns 200. Because history is linear, that one deployment carried the whole email feature — the two blocked commits needed no re-doing. **The two blocked deployments are superseded; do not try to "re-run" them.**
  **The generalisable lesson:** a deployment that is silently *blocked* looks exactly like a deployment that never triggered, and both look like "the push didn't deploy". **Check the deployment status, not the push exit code** — `vercel ls <project>` shows `Ready` vs `Blocked` at a glance, and `vercel inspect` gives the reason verbatim.
  **⚠️ CORRECTIONS TO EARLIER NOTES IN THIS FILE:** the deploy gap was **not** a wrong-project problem. `prj_9vFt3EYmZTTFzWiUpUCwb5cMHzGd` / `team_Q0jg76B4cOO3Divzr8zNL3Hk` in `.vercel/project.json` is stale, but the real project is **`drop-in`** under `jonmeisburgs-projects` and it **is** correctly wired to GitHub — pushes do trigger builds. Also, the Vercel CLI token that read as `invalidToken: true` was simply **expired** (`expiresAt` 2026-09-25 21:34 UTC); the CLI refreshed it and `whoami` returns **`jonmeisburg`**. **The founder is not locked out of Vercel** — only of the Supabase dashboard.

  **WHAT STILL WORKS (measured, not assumed):** the project is `ACTIVE_HEALTHY`; the token **reads AND writes** the Management API (`GET` and `PATCH /v1/projects/<ref>/config/auth` both return 200 — the 401 seen first was a *validation* error, not auth: *"Custom SMTP required to configure SMTP_SENDER_NAME or RATE_LIMIT_EMAIL_SENT. Missing SMTP_ADMIN_EMAIL, SMTP_HOST, SMTP_PORT, SMTP_USER, SMTP_PASS fields."* — i.e. the rate limit cannot be set without the SMTP fields in the SAME request); project secrets are readable; **SQL still runs**, which is how migration 0053 was applied. - **🚀 `send-push` DEPLOYED AND PROVEN RUNNING — THE EMAIL-FALLBACK ENGINEERING IS COMPLETE (2026-09-26).** Deployed with `npx --yes supabase@2.118.0 functions deploy send-push --project-ref …`, authenticated by `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` (no interactive login; `package.json` and `node_modules` untouched). **Verified by three independent probes rather than by the CLI's success message:** Management API shows `version` **2 → 3** and `ezbr_sha256` **`54e36b98…` → `737a68d9…`** with `verify_jwt: true` and `ACTIVE`; the anon probe still returns **401 `{"error":"send-push is service-role only"}`** byte-identical to pre-deploy (proves the function serves and the auth wall holds without draining a row); and the 15:05:00 cron tick returned **`status_code: 200, timed_out: false`** in `net._http_response`.

- **🚨 REPO-INTEGRITY HAZARD — `reuseExistingServer: true` CAN SERVE ANOTHER WORKTREE'S BUILD, AND A GREEN E2E RUN MAY HAVE TESTED CODE THAT DOES NOT EXIST HERE (2026-09-26, found while adding `e2e/email-optout.e2e.ts`).** **Read this before trusting ANY e2e result from this machine.**
  **The mechanism.** `playwright.config.ts:54-69` sets `webServer.reuseExistingServer: true` and justifies it in a comment: *"V22 slice 12: rebuild before serving, so a reused server can never hand the suite a STALE dist/. … Building first makes reuse safe: the server restarts on the fresh output."* **That reasoning is wrong.** With `reuseExistingServer: true`, if port 4173 is already answering, Playwright **never runs the `command` at all** — so `npm run build` does not execute, nothing restarts, and the suite is served whatever that process was started from. If the process belongs to a **different worktree**, the suite silently tests a *different revision of the app*.
  **It is not hypothetical — it happened on the first run.** `/home/jmeisburg/Projects/playdate-app.worktrees/v25` had a `vite preview` up on :4173 (an older build with **no email opt-out block at all**). The new spec's first run reused it. It was caught only because the agent checked the serving process's cwd; **on this machine there are at least two other worktrees** (`v25`, `cpilot-free-usage-query`), so any of them can do this at any time.
  **Consequence for prior results, stated honestly:** the targeted `e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts` run recorded earlier in this batch (**8 passed**) was taken **without** first confirming what was on :4173, so that result was **unverified at the time**. It has since been **re-run with :4173 confirmed free and passed 8/8 (exit 0, 33.0s)**, so it stands — but it stood by luck the first time, not by method. The `e2e/email-optout.e2e.ts` run (**3 passed**) *was* taken with :4173 confirmed free, so it is sound.
  **Detection (run this before believing an e2e result):** resolve the listener's real working directory and compare it to yours — `ss -ltnp | grep :4173`, then `readlink -f /proc/<pid>/cwd`. If it is not this worktree, **the run tested the wrong app**.
  **The fix is NOT applied, deliberately.** `playwright.config.ts` is `config-guard`-protected (`scripts/guards/config-guard.sh:62`), so changing it needs `ALLOW_CONFIG_CHANGE="<why>"` **and a human decision** — and that guard exists precisely so a gate cannot be quietly adjusted. Candidate fixes, best first: (a) derive the preview **port from the worktree** so two worktrees can never collide; (b) make reuse conditional on the serving process's cwd matching the current worktree; (c) `reuseExistingServer: false` (errors out loudly if the port is taken, which is at least honest). **Per invariant 9, do not add another standalone preflight script that nothing invokes — this needs a real wiring decision, not a lane that rots.**

- **🗄️ MIGRATION `0053` APPLIED LIVE + A NEW EDGE-FUNCTION CHECK LANE (2026-09-26).** `profiles.email_optout` now exists in project `ayzvjwxbxyrcgyoeaxuk`: `boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, **0 of 438 rows opted out**, and a re-apply of the same file exits 0 with no change — idempotence proven against the live database, not argued from the SQL. Applied via `bash scripts/db-sql.sh --file …` (**browserless**); `apply-migration.mjs` would have visibly navigated the human's own Chrome, which browser-lanes.md §7 forbids without an explicit yes.
  **NEW LANE — `scripts/deno-check-functions.sh`.** `npm run typecheck` is `tsc -b` and does **not** cover `supabase/functions/` (Deno globals + `npm:` specifiers are unresolvable to tsc). So the email-fallback slice's `drain()` rewrite was verified by "typecheck plus inspection" — which meant **tsc had never seen the file**. `deno` 2.9.6 is installed here, so this script type-checks every entrypoint in its real runtime: `send-push` and `prefill-playdate` both PASS. It stages a temp copy instead of adding `supabase/functions/deno.json`, because that file would change how the **Supabase CLI bundles on deploy**. Exits 0 when deno is absent, so it cannot become a hard CI dependency. **It is STANDALONE — `package.json` is `config-guard`-protected (`scripts/guards/config-guard.sh:62`), so wiring it into `verify` needs `ALLOW_CONFIG_CHANGE="<why>"` and a human decision. Per invariant 9 it must be adopted or it will rot.**
  **`send-push` IS DELIBERATELY NOT DEPLOYED.** With no `RESEND_API_KEY`/`EMAIL_FROM` the email branch is unreachable, so a deploy delivers zero user value today while risking a live function serving 438 profiles. Production is *consistent*: DB has the inert column, app and function unchanged. Deploy atomically with the secrets. **Blast radius proven if that changes:** the diff is 154 insertions / **5 deletions**, all accounted for — three comment rewordings and two code lines (`.update({…'no subscription'})`, `skipped += 1`) reproduced **verbatim** inside the new `if (!EMAIL_ENABLED)` branch; the push-send/prune path is untouched. Targeted `e2e/push-subscribe.e2e.ts`: **8 passed, 33.7s**.

A **drift guard** now asserts `EMAIL_KINDS` equals `NOTIFICATION_KINDS` so that class of divergence cannot recur. **Pin before dispatch, but treat a builder that contradicts a pin as evidence to verify, never as noise to override.**

- **🔎 SEAM DECISION — PARENT CARDS + LINKED PARENT ON THE PROFILE READ VIEW (2026-09-25, base `c286b83`).** The delegated question: should `/u/:handle`'s read view surface parent cards and the linked-parent line, or do they stay edit-only? **Decision: keep both EDIT-ONLY. Do NOT extend `profileBlurbOrder`'s read surface for either block.** Rationale, in one sentence each:
  1. **Parent cards:** RLS *permits* a third-party read (`parent_cards_select_authenticated` = `to authenticated using (true)`), so it is technically possible — but V19 t05 pinned them to `/profile` only ("the profile renders two parent cards" on the edit surface), and the read view's privacy posture (kids hidden from strangers, family photo gated) makes surfacing named+photographed parents to any signed-in stranger a NEW privacy surface the founder never asked for. Extending it is a product decision, not a mechanical one; absent a founder ask, the conservative default is to leave the read view as-is.
  2. **Linked parent:** RLS *forbids* a third-party read (`account_links_select_parties` = the two parties only; a stranger gets ZERO ROWS). Surfacing "linked to @partner" on a stranger's read view would require a NEW migration widening that policy — a significant privacy-surface change with no founder request behind it. Not doing it.
  - **Net:** no code change, no migration, no `profileBlurbOrder` extension. The seam stays as-is: `parentCards`+`linkedParent` pushed only when `surface==='edit'`. This is a *decision to do nothing*, recorded so a future session does not re-derive it or accidentally "fix" the read view to show these blocks. If the founder later asks to show parent cards on the public face, that is a new ticket (RLS already permits it); showing linked-parent would additionally need a DB policy change.

- **✅ FIRST-USE DISCOVERY AUDIT IMPLEMENTED — ALL FIVE TICKETS (`9082415`, 2026-09-25).** The captain handed over `docs/handoff-first-use-discovery-audit-2026-09-25.md` and asked for `/to-tickets` then implementation. The brief's four prioritized items became **five** tickets at `.scratch/first-use-discovery-audit/issues/`, because the captain's own answer to Q4 ("a failed sweep fails `npm run verify`") split the fixture work into a deterministic repo half and a live operational half — a guard that needs live Supabase credentials to pass is a guard that gets skipped, which is the bug being fixed. **Execution was direct, not dispatched**: the repo's orchestrator spine is the stated policy for product batches, and this session is not running it; that deviation is recorded here rather than presented as the normal path.

  **WHAT SHIPPED.** *01* the `/* V23 slice 3 … */` comment rendered below the Post button on `/new`, with a RENDERED-text regression assertion (red first, then green). *02* the silent ZIP fallback, plus `src/lib/geocode.test.ts` (14 tests) and the removal of that module's lib-sibling exemption. *03* origin-aware deferral of the push prompt off the RSVP confirmation. *04* `scripts/guards/fixture-marker-guard.mjs` + `docs/agents/e2e-fixture-convention.md` + a `.check.mjs` that proves the guard fires. *05* the sweep's self-report and self-proof, its decision extracted to `scripts/lib/sweep-e2e.mjs` with its own checker, and its promotion to a required step in `docs/beta-checklist.md`.

  **THE MOST IMPORTANT FINDING WAS NOT IN THE AUDIT — IT WAS BEHIND IT.** Ticket 02's fix exposed that **the signup address→ZIP write had NEVER worked**: `LoginPage` computed `session?.user.id ?? ''` from the render that ran *before* signup, so the write hit `profiles?id=eq.` and PostgREST 400'd on an empty uuid. Every new parent with a resolvable address was therefore sent to the ZIP screen — which is exactly the "it asked me for my location twice" friction the audit reported, now with a cause instead of a symptom. `e2e/auth.setup.ts` found it from the other side when the fix landed: the setup spec waited for a screen the parent should never have seen, and timed out. **Lesson: the audit's "minor friction" can be a broken write, and the fix to a fallback is worth nothing until you check the happy path actually runs.**

  **EVIDENCE.** `npm run verify` exit 0 (build + 1268 unit tests + lint + a11y focus + steering-lint + guards). Full e2e suite: **117 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed / 0 flaky, 9.5m** (`6fb192b`, the commit after the fix below). Targeted: `place-directory-in-new` 5 passed, `push-subscribe` 8 passed, `signup-zip-fallback` 2 passed + setup.

  **A SECOND, LARGER FALLOUT THE FIRST RUN CAUGHT — AND WHY IT MATTERED.** Ticket 02's fix made the resolved branch real, and ~19 existing specs had encoded the OLD behaviour: sign up, then unconditionally wait for "Set your location". Every one of them then hung for the full two-minute timeout waiting for a screen that no longer appears (the first full-suite run: 115 passed / 1 skipped / 1 flaky / **9 failed**). Fixed once in `e2e/fixtures.ts` as `finishSignup(page, { homeZip, radiusMiles })`, which waits for whichever branch settles first and fills the location step only when it is there — never a forced reload, so specs that count requests during a cold load still count only that load. **The lesson: making a fallback stop firing is a behaviour change to every test that asserted the fallback.** A green targeted run cannot see it; only the full suite can.

  **TWO SHAPE DECISIONS WORTH NOT RE-DERIVING.** The standalone checkers are `*.check.mjs`, NOT `*.test.mjs` — `npm test` discovers `*.test.mjs` and a top-level `process.exit()` kills the vitest runner, which is how the first attempt failed (`2 failed | 41 passed` → `41 passed` once renamed). And `steering-lint`'s 1800-word ceiling on `AGENTS.md` rejected three attempts to document the new guard; the fourth rule is in, paid for by pruning the skills section.

- **✅ ALL THREE QUEUED FIRSTMATE ITEMS ARE NOW CLOSED — `branch-row-routing-stall` FIXED AND PR'D (`Meisburg/firstmate#2`, `cd4a6fdb`, 2026-09-25).** The captain picked route (c) with "do it". **The bug, precisely:** when the branch actor holds a lane's lease, main cannot act on that lane *at all* — `bin/fm-lease.sh` refuses the claim (exit 6) and `bin/fm-control.sh` refuses lifecycle control — so any row the eligibility scan keeps main-only *for a branch-leased lane* is a row **nobody can act on**, and the lane is unrecoverable without a human. The scan kept a stale row for a decision-owned task (open needs-decision, or a `captain-held` last verb) out of the branch's claim set while the captain is attended — correct in itself — but it did so **without asking who can actually act on the lane.**

  **THE FIX.** `scopeForUnreadWake` (`.pi/extensions/lib/fm-branch-dispatch.ts`) now checks the lease first: when the lane's lease is held by a **live** branch actor, the branch is the only actor that may act, so its stale row stays claimable and no longer marks later triggers for the same task as main-only. The lease test mirrors `bin/fm-lease-lib.sh`'s `fm_lease_live` exactly (actor + numeric live holder pid + session lock naming the same pid), so a dead, torn or main-held lease file **never** takes a row away from main. Scoped to the **stale** path — the filed case, "a parked lane whose worker has stopped". The needs-decision **signal** path keeps its pinned main-only rule: a worker *asking a question* is the captain's to answer, and it is not the stranding case.

  **EVIDENCE, RED FIRST.** A new case in `tests/fm-supervision-host.test.sh` drives a stale row for a task with an open needs-decision through `bin/fm-branch-dispatch.mjs scope`. Unpatched, it reports **`status=unsafe` with an empty claim set even with a live branch lease** — the filed defect, reproduced — and patched it reports `rows=1 tasks=demo`. The case covers all four branches of the predicate (no lease, live branch lease, main lease, dead holder), so the rule cannot quietly widen. `bin/fm-test-run.sh tests/fm-supervision-host.test.sh` exit 0 / 0 failed; `bin/fm-lint.sh` exit 0 with pinned ShellCheck 0.11.0. Merge is the captain's call on his own fork, same as `#1`.

Two fork PRs await his merge: `Meisburg/firstmate#1` and `#2`.

- **🔴→✅ THE DESIGN PASS BROKE TWO E2E SPECS AND NO GATE COULD SEE IT — FOUND AND FIXED (`#12`, `3ce7898`, 2026-09-25).** This is the most important lesson of the whole batch. The six design-pass PRs were each validated with `npm run verify`, which **does not run e2e** — deliberately, because `test:e2e` signs up real accounts against the live Supabase project and is excluded from `verify.yml`. So nothing checked the four slices *together* against the full suite after they all merged. Running the full suite on **merged master** found **2 deterministic failures**, and both were caused by the design pass itself.

  **THE DEFECT CLASS: A SPEC ANCHORED ON STYLING.** `e2e/comment-replies.e2e.ts` (5 anchors) and `e2e/kids-v3.e2e.ts` (1) located their section with `locator('div.rounded-xl', { hasText: … })`. Slice D de-carded the Comments section and the "Kids coming" block from `rounded-xl border … shadow-sm` to plain `flex flex-col gap-3` sections, so those anchors matched **nothing** and both specs failed "element(s) not found". The product was correct; the selector was not. **A class name is not a contract** — the same lesson this file recorded for the V23 title rename, in a new costume.

  **THE FIX.** Stable `data-testid` attributes in `src/pages/PlaydateDetailPage.tsx` (`comments-section`, `kids-coming-line`), specs pointed at them. One trap worth recording: the first attempt tagged the **signed-out** Comments section (bare `Comments` heading) and the spec still failed, because the spec asserts the **counted** heading `Comments (N)`, which lives in the *signed-in* branch guarded by `state.comments !== null`. The tag belongs on that branch. **No anchor in `e2e/` still selects on a card class.**

  **EVIDENCE.** Full suite on the fix branch: **114 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed / 0 flaky, 9.1m, exit 0** — against 111 passed / 2 failed / 1 flaky on the pre-fix merged master. Targeted run of the two specs: 3 passed. `npm run verify` exit 0; CI verify green before merge.

  **THE METHOD RULE THIS ADDS.** A design/anchor-changing batch is NOT validated by per-slice `verify` runs. **After any batch that changes markup, run the FULL e2e suite once on merged master** — per-slice gates cannot see cross-slice and selector drift, and the nightly lane would have surfaced these as a red run hours later, attributed to whatever landed most recently. This is the batch-end e2e rule in `docs/agents/browser-lanes.md`, and it was skipped for slices A–D because their briefs deferred the live lane to the nightly schedule.

The failure was a genuine race in the shared `LocationModal`: its slider kept a local copy synced by `useEffect(..., [radiusMiles])`, so the first apply's `refresh()` landed mid-drag, reset the slider to the value just saved, and the second "Apply radius" wrote an equal value — a silent no-op with no error. Load-sensitive, which is why earlier runs never pinned it; proven pre-existing on clean master before fixing. Fixed in `091f934` by holding the user's in-session draft (`draftRadius`, falling back to the caller's saved value, cleared on open). **Final full-suite run after the fix: 114 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed, 9.0m, under load.**

- **✅ V23 FOLLOW-UP #3 — THE DM UNREAD DOT IS SHIPPED (2026-09-23, `50cd312`→`d2a64fc`, branch `fm/drop-in-dm-unread-dot`).** The founder decision the two "SCHEMA WALL" records below awaited was taken and built. **The design (the s7 recon's recommendation):** a SEPARATE cursor table — migration `0051` `direct_conversation_reads (profile_id, other_profile_id, last_read_at)`, composite PK, own-rows-only RLS (3 policies mirroring 0042), idempotent, applied live — so the working playdate path (`conversation_reads` and every query against it) is untouched byte-for-byte. **What changed:** `listDirectConversationsWithClient` computes a real DM unreadCount (messages FROM the counterpart newer than the viewer's 0051 cursor; the viewer's own sent messages never count); `markDirectConversationRead` stamps the cursor when a DM thread opens; the inbox dot + badge render for BOTH kinds; `e2e/dm.e2e.ts` pins the dot appearing and clearing on the recipient side. **The merge's unread rule (three adjudicated review rounds):** per counterpart, keep the newest DM row and the newest playdate row; a MIXED group displays the SUM of those two counts (the two cursors are disjoint, so the sum is exact); a single-kind group displays its newest row's own count; identity/preview/latestAt/kind stay the overall winner's, with the playdate row winning an exact tie. Owner: the `src/lib/inbox.ts` docstrings; pinned by `src/lib/inbox.test.ts` and `src/lib/db-messages.test.ts`. **Plus a pre-existing recipient-side defect the new e2e exposed, fixed here:** `queryDirectMessagesWithClient` read the OTHER party's `message_recipients` rows — hidden from a recipient by the SELECT policy — so a DM the other person STARTED opened as "No messages yet"; it reads the caller's own participation rows (`profile_id = myId`, populated for both parties by the 0044 trigger) now.

  *(This entry supersedes the DM-unread-gap claims in the V23 COMPLETE and paused-batch entries below: the gap is closed, not reported.)*

- **✅ V23 FOLLOW-UP — THE MAP FEEDBACK FIXES ARE IN THE TREE AND GREEN (2026-09-23).** Two founder reports on the places map: *"when i click on a blue circle … the map goes white"* and *"the red radius is going outside the map lol … the whole thing is not looking right."* Both fixed in `src/components/PlaceMap.tsx` + `src/index.css` + `src/lib/places.ts` (+4 unit tests, +1 e2e regression assertion).

  **WHITE MAP — a DOM-ownership collision, not map logic.** The container's `className` prop changed when the popup opened (it dropped `overflow-hidden`), so React rewrote the attribute and DELETED every `leaflet-*` class Leaflet had appended; `.leaflet-tile-loaded`'s `visibility: inherit` then resolved to Leaflet's sheet default `hidden` — tiles gone, white box. Fixed by freezing the className string and keeping `overflow-hidden` always on; the popup is held inside by `autoPan` + the CSS ceiling.

  **RADIUS OUTSIDE THE MAP — three causes, all required.** A stale 250px pane default (`zoomForRadius` was never told the 380px band), Leaflet flooring the fractional zoom (`zoomSnap` default 1), and latitude-blind Mercator arithmetic (1.48x too big at Seattle's 47.6°). Fixed by passing `map.getSize()`'s shorter axis, `zoomSnap: 0`, the `cos(lat)` correction + a fit margin, and `panTo` to centre. The popup ceilings were also too tight (the "Start a drop-in" button was scrolled out and its centre hit-tested the wrapper); raised to 34vh / 42vh.

  **EVIDENCE, measured on the current tree at 390x844 `/browse`** (`.scratch/v23/verify-founder-map.mjs`): circle inside the band on both axes (0.85 w / 0.74 h); after a marker tap `.leaflet-container` retained, 6 tiles visible, container bg `#ddd`, 0 pageerrors; popup contained with the button's centre hit-testing the BUTTON. `npm run verify` exit 0 — 39 files / 1195 tests, lint clean, a11y/steering/guards PASS.

  **NOT FIXED, RECORDED:** `places.e2e.ts`'s `learn-more` expectation is stale since V23 slice 4 removed the panel's map-search fallback; that spec is in the batch's pre-existing failure set.

  **AND THE OLD CI BLOCKER IS RESOLVED:** `gh auth status` now shows the `workflow` scope and `origin/master` is level with the local CI commits (`c29f185`); the "⏸️ BLOCKED" note below is historical, kept for the record.

- **⏸️ V23 CI — BUILT, COMMITTED (`d7d846d`), AND BLOCKED ON A CREDENTIAL SCOPE THE FOUNDER MUST GRANT (HISTORICAL — see the follow-up entry above).** One command clears it: `gh auth refresh -s workflow`. Then `git push origin master` (details, including the `ALLOW_CONFIG_CHANGE` export that is needed only while the commit is unpushed, are in `.scratch/v23/ledger.md`).

  **WHAT IT ADDS.** Until V23 this repo had **NO CI AT ALL** — no `.github/workflows`, no other pipeline. Every gate was real but lived on one machine. `.github/workflows/verify.yml` runs one command, `npm run verify`, deliberately NOT re-listing the stages (a second list would be a second definition of the gate drifting from `package.json`), plus the tracked-hook install the `no-bypass` guard asserts. Full operational doc: `docs/agents/ci.md`.

  **THE SECRETS DECISION, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED.** The build needs `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` + `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (confirmed by moving `.env` aside and watching `verify` fail). They are **repository variables, not secrets** — the anon key is compiled into the shipped bundle, and what protects the data is RLS: probed with the anon key, `profiles`, `messages`, `place_comments`, `parent_cards` each return **zero rows** while `places` returns the public directory. Missing variables SKIP the job with a notice rather than failing, because a red X for "not configured yet" teaches people to ignore red Xs.

  **TWO REAL CI BLOCKERS FOUND BY SIMULATING A FRESH CLONE** (git clone with no `.env`, no `node_modules`, then `npm ci` + the gate — three failures were found and fixed, not by reading the YAML): (1) **steering-lint pointed at `supabase/.temp/linked-project.json`**, GITIGNORED machine-local CLI state that CANNOT exist in a clone — the first CI run this repo ever had would have failed on a file CI cannot create; added to its `ALLOW_ABSENT` list, purely additive, and verified the lint still catches a genuinely stale pointer. (2) **A test-count divergence:** local said 40 files / 1197 tests, the clone 39 / 1191 — because `.scratch/` is gitignored but still ON DISK and vitest discovers tests by WALKING THE TREE rather than asking git, so a preserved `inbox.test.ts` copy from the concurrent-writer incident ran as an extra file CI could never have. Deleted (committed originals byte-identical). **Local and CI now both report 39/1191.**

  **VERIFIED ON THE COMMITTED TREE:** a clone of `d7d846d`, run exactly as CI runs it, exits 0 — 39/1191, 0 lint errors, steering PASS, guards PASS. Also confirmed the `config-guard` will NOT fire in CI: it uses `merge-base origin/master HEAD`, so once the commit is on origin the check-config change is in the baseline.

  **THE BLOCKER IS A SCOPE, NOT LABOUR:** GitHub refused the push — `refusing to allow an OAuth App to create or update workflow '.github/workflows/verify.yml' without 'workflow' scope`. `gh auth status` shows the token has `gist`, `read:org`, `repo` — **no `workflow`**. The pre-push gate itself PASSED. Only the account owner can grant a scope, so this is one command for the founder: `gh auth refresh -s workflow`.

- **✅ V23 WRAP-UP COMPLETE (2026-09-23). PUSHED as `8edfdf0` and `bf95ea5`.** Everything outstanding is closed: the order check now seeds real content, the mobile audit ran over this batch's surfaces, and migration 0050 was re-verified on the committed tree.

  **THE WRAP-UP FOUND A REAL USER-FACING BUG NO EARLIER LANE HAD SEEN: `/place/:id` RENDERED A BLANK PAGE.** The mobile audit (which had never covered this surface — `mobile-audit.mjs` sweeps a hardcoded unauthenticated `['/login','/playdate/:id','/browse']`) reported an uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')` with an EMPTY BODY. Root cause: `PlaceMapLazy.tsx` had ONE lazy factory resolving through the module's DEFAULT export — which is `PlacePickerMap` — cast to `ComponentType<any>`, and used by all three wrappers. So `PlaceMap`, which passes `place`, rendered the PICKER, which reads `places.map(...)`. **The `as unknown as ComponentType<any>` cast erased the prop mismatch TypeScript would have caught: a compile-time-safe lie.** The V22 note in that file records fixing an earlier crash (React #306) by ADDING a default export — which fixed the UNDEFINED component while introducing the WRONG one. **Proven pre-existing** by building base `ff49d0c` in an isolated worktree and hitting the same id: identical pageerror, identical blank body. Fixed with one lazy per NAMED export and casts that name the real props type; verified all three surfaces render their own component with 0 pageerrors (`/place/:id` map, `/browse` 116 markers, `/new` 241 markers).

  **THE ORDER CHECK NOW SEEDS WHAT IT MEASURES — AND FIXING THE VACUITY EXPOSED TWO BUGS IN THE CHECK ITSELF.** It uploads a real 1x1 PNG to `<uid>/family/order-check-fixture.png`, PATCHes a bio + that path, measures, then restores and deletes — self-seeding and self-cleaning, verified afterwards from the DB (`bio=null`, no fixture object). The read side is now `["parents","familyPhoto"]` against a real editor list. Both bugs it exposed had been **unreachable because the branch never ran**: (1) the read-side probe inserted `familyPhoto` BEFORE `parents`, the opposite of `ProfileView.tsx:571`, so once seeding worked the check **failed against a correct page and accused the editor**; (2) the assertion compared **absolute indexes across different-length projections** and could only ever have passed vacuously. Both rewritten as the actual property and proven in both directions — passes on the tree, fails on the injected pre-fix drift.

  **MOBILE AUDIT: 40 findings, 2 distinct elements, BOTH PRE-EXISTING CHROME.** The 24x44 Settings gear (`App.tsx:237`, 44px tall but 24px wide) and a 151x42 "Browse places" link in the inbox empty state (`InboxPage.tsx:882`, 2px under). **Proven identical at base**, after a false start worth recording: the first base measurement returned EMPTY for every route and would have "proved" the base clean — but the saved session is bound to origin `localhost:4173` and the routes had all redirected to `/login`. **An empty result from a page that did not load is not evidence of absence** — the same lesson as the `[]`-vs-populated comparison this batch hit three times. Re-ran the setup spec inside the worktree, served base on 4173 so the origin matched, and got byte-identical findings. **Not fixed here** (outside the batch's scope; recorded with exact selectors as open work).

  **MIGRATION 0050 RE-VERIFIED ON THE COMMITTED TREE:** 4 policies, RLS on, 0 rows, and anon still reads `[]`.

  **AUDIT SCRIPTS WRITTEN, DELIBERATELY NOT WIRED INTO `verify`:** `.scratch/v23/mobile-audit-v23.mjs` covers this batch's five surfaces at 4 phone widths x 2 appearances. It is separate from the standing `mobile-audit.mjs` because extending that lane would change what it covers — a different decision from auditing one batch. **The manual-lane gap is therefore still open and still recorded:** `a11y:profile-order` needs a browser + preview server + signed-in account, so it stays a manual lane.

Kept rather than discarded because the defect is real, the work is tested, and provenance is not a reason to destroy verified code. **Committed separately and labelled as having skipped this batch's lanes**, so no future reader assumes it passed them.   **ITS GUARD HAD A REAL DEFECT AND IT IS THE BATCH'S FOURTH INSTANCE OF THE SAME CLASS.** `scripts/profile-order-check.mjs` **passed vacuously**: the marker has no bio/kids/photo, so the read view renders zero shared blocks, and the cross-surface check compared `[]` against the editor's list and reported agreement for ANY editor order. **Proven by injecting `readShared = []`** and watching both checks print `ok`. Fixed: the read projection must now be a subsequence of the EDIT projection (the editor is genuinely the other half, not a bystander in its own log line), plus a **vacuity floor** that fails when the read side is empty while the profile has content. Both directions proven — the floor fails on an injected empty read side; the order check fails on an injected drift. **One thing I got wrong:** I first suspected the `main h2` selector was broken; probing showed `ProfileView.tsx` does emit those h2s and the account is simply empty. The selector was fine — the vacuity was the bug.

  **THE GUARDS FIRED TWICE AND WERE BOTH RIGHT.** `config-guard` blocked the package.json change ("this is how a failing gate gets silently disabled") and was answered with an `ALLOW_CONFIG_CHANGE` reason, with `verify`'s command list confirmed byte-identical. The pre-push hook then blocked the push because it runs `verify` in its own environment and could not see the reason — correct behaviour; the fix was exporting the reason, **not** `FAST_PUSH=1`. The guard's own doc says "do not silence the guard"; that was followed.

(2) Slice 6's first metric measured `place-marker-info`'s bounding box — a **scrolled child**, which reports its natural height even while visibly clipped — and said "284px, unchanged" across TWO fix attempts while the CSS was working; the correct metric is the POPUP element against the band. Fixed in CSS where it binds: ratio **0.79–0.83 → 0.53** at three viewports, 106px of map visible above the bubble — and the counter-claim checked: all three controls remain **44px and centre-hit-testable** after scrolling.

  **THE BATCH'S RECURRING DEFECT CLASS: a check passed while the property did not hold. Four times.** (1) Slice 1's acceptance criterion was "grep for ≥15 literal `aria-describedby`" — the attribute comes from a `{...fieldA11y()}` spread and exists only at RUNTIME, so the criterion would have failed a CORRECT implementation; the builder flagged it instead of gaming the grep. (2) Slice 5's evidence was `grep activeElement`, which proves the hook is imported, not that it traps. (3) Slice 7's safety check `grep -v focus-visible` cannot distinguish `focus-visible:outline-none` (NO cue) from `focus-visible:outline-none focus-visible:ring-2` (fine) — it reported "zero matches" and was wrong; the real defect was `InboxPage.tsx:949`, pre-existing as `focus:outline-none`. (4) Slice 10 proved the chunk shrank AND that `/login` never fetches it — both true, both compatible with the lazy component resolving to `undefined`. **Each fix was the same: replace the proxy with a real assertion.** New permanent gates came out of it (`scripts/focus-indicator-check.mjs` wired into `verify`; `a11y-dom-check.mjs`, `dark-mode-check.mjs`, `layout-width-check.mjs`, `focus-trap-check.mjs` as manual lanes), each PROVEN TO FAIL before being trusted.

  **FOUR DEFECTS FOUND IN ALREADY-"VERIFIED" WORK — the pattern matters more than the bugs.** (1) **Slice 9 broke the PUBLIC SHARE SURFACE**: the desktop grid applied unconditionally while the nav rail renders only when `session !== null`, so `/playdate/:id` — the one page a signed-out visitor ever sees — collapsed to a **72px** column at 1024px. Slice 9's own audit swept phone widths only, where the grid is inactive, and its layout measurements were signed-in. Found by an orchestrator browser probe; fixed by making the grid conditional on the same `session` check. (2) **Slice 10 shipped a crash**: `lazy(() => import('./PlaceMap'))` resolved to `undefined` (three NAMED exports, no default) → React #306 on every map surface, `/new` rendered nothing. Found by slice 12's builder, fixed with a default export, now covered by the e2e spec's `.leaflet-container` assertion. (3) **`reuseExistingServer: true` could run e2e against a STALE `dist/`** (test:e2e does not build) — found by the repo's own NEW `config-guard` on its first live run; fixed by adding the build to the webServer command rather than silencing the guard. (4) **Slice 9's builder reverted `ProfileView.tsx` to HEAD**, discarding slice 11's `eager` fix, to keep its own build green — restored by the orchestrator; recorded because "keep my build green" is not license to discard another slice's landed work.

  **TWO AUDIT CLAIMS RETRACTED, both by the orchestrator, both after a builder pushed back rather than manufacturing a change to satisfy a brief.** (a) *"Mockups live in production"* (`AUDIT.md`): FALSE — the two HTTP 200s were Vercel's SPA fallback (`vercel.json` rewrites every path to `/index.html`); the served body was byte-identical to `dist/index.html` (4747 B) while the real files are 10886/11777 B. They were gitignored on purpose, never committed, never served. Latent risk, not exposure. A **status code is not evidence about content when a rewrite rule is in play.** (b) *"Mixed button capitalization"* (`DESIGN-REVIEW.md` finding 13): FALSE — both cited strings are instructional prose (`push.ts:181`) and a native `<option>` (`PlaceDirectory.tsx:647`). A scan of every `<button>`/`submitLabel` found **0** title-case labels; the app was already consistently sentence-case. Slice 13 correctly reported "no changes needed".

  **THE HUMAN'S DECISIONS (both asked mid-batch, both recorded in `plan.md` Risks):** dark mode shipped as *insurance, not a headline feature* ("if they want to use it, they can use it") — the palette was verified by sampling PAINTED pixels (dark page `rgb(24,20,18)`, card `rgb(36,31,28)`, terracotta link 6.63:1 — matching the builder's independently computed table exactly) and reviewed as screenshots; and the FULL rail + wide content was chosen over a cheaper "just widen it" option, with pixel-equivalence at 390px as the safety property.

  **DELIBERATELY NOT DONE (decided, not dropped):** swipe-to-go-back (the browser owns the gesture in a web PWA; the tap alternative already exists), an app-level appearance toggle (Apple explicitly warns against one), `GoingCircle` display names (the feed type carries no name; a data-shape change with 351 dependent tests), and further entry-chunk splitting (React + Supabase + router, not the map).

  **OPEN, FILED RATHER THAN DROPPED:** a 320px overflow on the signed-in feed (Distance select + home-zip Save form) — proven pre-existing by reproducing on baseline; the mobile audit only sweeps signed-out routes, which is why nothing caught it. And the `scripts/*-check.mjs` lanes are manual — they need a server and (mostly) a signed-in session, so they wire into `verify` once an e2e fixture account exists in CI.
